# BlueAgent inventory, measured (2026-09-29)

This is a read-only audit of what BlueAgent actually has. It was measured from the repo at HEAD `5bd8c50f`, from production (`https://blueagent.dev`, `https://app.blueagent.dev`) and from Base 8453 on-chain, and it replaces an inventory assembled from the outside. The rule behind every number: derive it from the source that would change if it were wrong, never copy it from a doc, a test, a comment or a memory note. Where two sources disagree, both are shown along with which one is trusted and why. Where something could not be measured, the file says so and gives the reason. A missing row is never a zero.

## How this was measured

- **Tree.** The deployed code is the committed tree (`git ls-files`, `git show HEAD:<path>`). The working tree holds someone else's uncommitted, undeployed work: `apps/web/src/app/api/flash/`, `apps/web/src/lib/flash/`, `apps/web/src/app/chat/components/FlashTradeCard.tsx`, and local edits to `api/chat/route.ts`, `api/chat/system-prompt.ts` and `chat/components/ToolCards.tsx`. Every row about that work is filed under **LOCAL-ONLY**. `research/` was not touched.
- **Production.** Requests were sequential, at least 0.5 s apart, with a 20 s timeout, and every redirect hop was recorded with its status and Location. No pass recorded a rate-limited response (section 1a: 0 of 190).
- **On-chain.** Base 8453 was read through `mainnet.base.org`: `eth_getLogs` in 2,000-block chunks (0 failed chunks reported), `eth_call balanceOf` bisection, receipts and `eth_getCode`. Robinhood Chain 4663 was read through `rpc.mainnet.chain.robinhood.com`. Blockscout was used for hints only, because it turned out to be incomplete (section 7).
- **Nothing written, paid or signed.** No `X-PAYMENT` header, no POST to a payment, credit, top-up or ACP route, no `tools/call` on an LLM-backed MCP tool, no deploy, no env pull, no secret value read. This file is the only thing written in the repo.
- **Last touched.** `git log -1` on the path. If that commit changed more than 20 files it is marked SWEEP, and the latest commit that changed 20 files or fewer is also given. A recent commit date is not evidence that a surface is alive.
- **Passes.** Nine measuring passes: 1a pages, 1b API routes, 2a tool counts, 2b per-tool detail, 3 scheduled tasks, 4 metering, 5 background work, 6 dead surface, 7 external users. Independent verification passes re-measured 2a, 2b, 3 and 4, and re-measured 7 twice: Skeptic A tried to tie every payer to self, and Skeptic B searched for a missed external payer. Sections 1a, 1b, 5 and 6 had no verification pass. Verifier corrections are applied in place and marked `corrected in verification: old -> new (evidence)`. A final fixer pass (2026-09-29, 09:54 to 10:10Z) closed 14 named gaps: it measured the off-repo schedulers, the storefront's source repo, the npm registry list, the 15 non-page route files, four dynamic URLs and eleven missing last-touched values, and re-read the evidence on Virtuals and KV. Its additions are marked `fixer pass`.
- **Rows.** 690 rows: 1a 87, 1b 150, 1c 16, 2a 36, 2b 147, 3 27, 4 35, 5 35, 6 114, 7 43. Every table has the columns name, what it does, measured status, evidence and last touched. Rows sit under sub-headings by the class of evidence behind them, not by folder. Evidence that names a scratch file (for example `s2a_import.ts` or `router_payments.json`) refers to the audit session's scratchpad, which is not committed.

**Totals.** 4 MCP `tools/call` requests (none reached an LLM, $0 charged). 2 metered GETs on `/api/hood/dislocation*` under the self-audit key. 5 unpaid GETs on `/api/x402/<id>` (2 returned 402, 3 returned 404). 3 MCP `tools/list` requests. 1 page render that triggered an internal POST to a deleted route. 0 `X-PAYMENT` headers. 0 POSTs to payment, credit, top-up or ACP routes. Separately, 33 unpaid GETs went to the third-party Bankr storefront.

**Every other request was a read-only GET with no counter**:

Off-host, the fixer pass read the GitHub API (repo, workflow, run and secret-name lists for `madebyshun/*`, and two shallow clones into the scratchpad), the npm registry and download API, and made keyless probes of `api.relay.link`, `api.0x.org` and `eth.llamarpc.com`. Off-host, the passes read `api.acp.virtuals.io`, the `api.cdp.coinbase.com` x402 discovery list, `x402scan.com`, `base.blockscout.com` and a Solana RPC, and made read-only RPC calls on Base and Robinhood Chain.

## 7 · The number that matters most

This section comes first because it outranks everything below it.

**Verdict: no payer on any BlueAgent paid surface is proven external. On the surfaces that take money today, every payment ever made came from ShunTr's own wallets.** Those surfaces are the x402 payee 0x0295...9205, Blue Chat credit top-ups and the ACP seller 0x884F...7A9B.

What does exist is external-looking evidence that nothing ties to self. All of it is small, and most of it is old:

- **blueagent.dev x402, retired payee 0xb058...3b5F (through the CDP facilitator), 2026-05-31 to 2026-07-05:** 8 wallets made 14 settlements totalling 1.60 USDC. Skeptic A showed that 5 of the 8 wallets are one operator (cluster A: 11 of the 14 settlements, 1.10 USDC), so there are at most 4 independent payers.
- **Bankr x402 Cloud storefront, forwarded to 0xf31f59, 2026-04-04 to 2026-09-19:** the measurer found 6 wallets, 11 settlements and 4.55 USDC. Skeptic A moved 0x01d80ecf (2 settlements, 0.10 USDC) to self-leaning (weak evidence), which leaves 5 wallets, 9 settlements and 4.45 USDC.
- **Together, 13 wallets, 23 settlements and 6.05 USDC are not refuted as self. None of them is proven external.**

The last unattributed payment into any blueagent.dev payee was on 2026-07-05 (0xd9fed0a2, 0.1 USDC). Since then, one unattributed payment has reached any BlueAgent-branded paid surface: 0.05 USDC through the Bankr storefront on 2026-09-19. The payer, 0x81b73786, was set up that day, registered on the ERC-8004 Identity Registry, then paid BlueAgent and another seller, so it leans external. The repo believes that storefront is gone (section 6). None of the unattributed wallets ever paid the current payee 0x0295 or the ACP seller.

**Why the 13 lean external. This is evidence, not proof:**

- **Buyer behaviour.** On x402scan, self wallets only ever paid BlueAgent payees. The unattributed wallets buy from many sellers: 0xb6c6ff19 made 73 tx to 28 sellers, 0xf7d1b9f8 36 to 15, 0xd9fed0a2 30 to 4, 0xdf7ed8b4 27 to 11 and 0xce971c02 17 to 6. x402scan's totals match the RPC count exactly: 0xb058 at 145 tx, 36.16 USDC and 11 buyers, and 0x0295 at 2 tx, 0.30 USDC and 2 buyers.
- **Device clock.** Two cluster-A wallets signed payment windows of 339 to 346 s against a 300 s window, so their signer's clock ran at least 39 s fast. ShunTr's 0xD5C1 never went past 296 s in 112 payments.
- **No link to self.** The scan covered every ERC-20 and ERC-721 transfer into or out of the 13 self and 16 candidate addresses, in both directions and two hops deep (blocks 42,766,926 to 51,939,572, 117,655 logs, 0 failed chunks). It found no direct transfer except the 14 known payments into 0xb058. All 26 shared intermediaries are infrastructure: routers, pools, bridges, Bankr deposit addresses, solvers and address poisoners. No candidate appears in `git log --all -p` (3,548 commits) or in 17 other local repos.
- **Client fingerprint.** The 14 CDP-era payments carry validAfter=0 and validBefore at about now+300. That matches BlueAgent's own Hub signer or `@blueagent/x402@0.1.0`, not a generic x402 crawler, so these payers came through BlueAgent's own client.

**Why it is not proof.** The chain cannot prove who owns a wallet. The scans do not cover native ETH, activity before 2026-03-01, or self wallets outside the 9 known ones.

**Other places a real user could have shown up:**

- **Logs:** Vercel runtime logs returned 403 and were not read.
- **Analytics:** `/api/stats` reports 99 runs and `revenueEst $5.51`. That counter also counts free MCP bypass calls (`mcp/route.ts:286`), so it is not revenue. At least 1 of the 99 is the audit's own: the self-audit `tools/call blue_doctor` at 06:22:35Z (ledger #3) succeeded, and a success increments `usage:blue-doctor` (`mcp/route.ts:286`; the two 502 calls throw at :283, before the increment). `/api/usage`, read at 06:28:37Z, shows blue-doctor 1. So at most 98 runs predate the audit. `/api/usage` also returns 8 console-command runs that are outside the 99: blue_idea 4, blue_build 3 and blue_audit 1 (`usage:blue_<cmd>`, `console/route.ts:63`; scratchpad `s7/prod_api_usage.json`). They are free and unattributed. MCP can reach only blue_build and blue_audit (`CONSOLE_MAP`, `mcp/route.ts:153-156`), so at least the 4 blue_idea runs came from another `/api/console` caller.
- **KV:** per-payer receipts `spend:<payer>` are private (90-day TTL), and `/api/usage/daily` returned 401.
- **Free credits, which are not a paid surface:** 29 airdrop claims, and 23 wallets spent 12,590 credits across 117 messages. Identities are not public, so how many are external is unknown.
- **Supply side:** 4 builder wallets outside the self set registered 6 tools on Blue Hub. They supply tools; they are not paying users.
- **npm downloads:** last month core had 240, skill 162, cli 97 and so on. These counts mix mirrors, scanners and CI, so they are not evidence of a user.

| paid surface | payee | window | payments (USDC) | self | not refuted as self | reading |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| blueagent.dev x402, retired payee | 0xb058...3b5F | 2026-05-29 to 2026-08-18 | 145 (36.16) | 131 (34.56): 0xD5C1 111, Blue Hub deployer 0x62b45ff0 17, 0x8eba37ef 3 | 14 (1.60) from 8 wallets, at most 4 operators; last on 2026-07-05 | external-looking, unproven |

In this table, 131 = 145 - 14 and 34.56 = 36.16 - 1.60, both derived from the section 7 rows. The detailed payer rows are in section 7, below the other sections.

## 1 · Surfaces

### What the evidence supports

**Pages.** HEAD has 77 committed `page.tsx` files (`git ls-files 'apps/web/src/app/**/page.tsx'`), 0 route groups and 0 local-only pages. The pass made 190 sequential GETs against production and followed every redirect hop by hand. The `x-matched-path` header shows which page file actually rendered.

| outcome in production | page files |
|---|---|
| live, linked from a nav | 27 |
| live, linked, but not from a nav | 17 |
| live, linked from nowhere (orphan) | 13 |
| never rendered by production (redirect stubs, plus 8 root `/hub/*` pages that middleware redirects before the page runs) | 14 |
| 404: every `/app/b20hub/*` page | 6 |
| **total** | **77** |

The three dynamic pages that were first left unprobed were measured in the fixer pass with a dummy value (`audit-probe-none`), the same way `/app/hub/builders/[address]` was: `/app/hub/registry/[handle]` and `/share/[id]` resolve and render (200), and `/hub/registry/[handle]` 301s to the app-host twin, so its own file never renders. The rows table below has 87 rows: the 77 pages plus 10 check rows (nav checks, the sitemap, middleware redirect sources, `/app/scheduled` and the local-only note).

- **Rendered but linked from nowhere (13):** `/stats`, `/docs/cli`, `/app/hub/stats`, `/app/hub/builders/[address]`, `/app/radar`, `/app/trade`, `/app/tasks`, `/app/bridge`, `/app/robinhood-router`, `/app/rewards`, `/waitlist`, `/builder/[handle]`, `/agent/[handle]`. "Orphan" means no internal link. No traffic data was read.
- **Nav items that 404 or redirect:** only three nav items fail to reach 200 directly.
  - AppShell's 'Docs' link (`/docs/blue-chat`, `AppShell.tsx:172`) and the Hood sidebar's docs link (`/docs/blue-hood`, `HoodSidebar.tsx:304`) each 301 from the app host to the main host, then load.
  - The b20hub layout's sub-nav (`layout.tsx:45-48`, four targets) all return 404, but that layout never renders in production.
  - Navbar's 'Launch app' 301s to the app host, which is by design. Every other AppShell, AccountMenu, HoodSidebar, DOC_NAV and Navbar href returns 200 with no hop.
- **B20HUB has returned 404 since the day it was created.** The chain is `blueagent.dev/app/b20hub` 301, then `app.blueagent.dev/b20hub` 301, then `blueagent.dev/b20hub` 404. `b20hub` is not in middleware `APP_SEGMENTS` and never was (`git log -S'"b20hub"' -- apps/web/src/middleware.ts` returns nothing). Meanwhile `scripts/action-card-inventory-check.ts:388-393` treats `app/app/b20hub/claim/ClaimClient.tsx` as the path that survived the Bankr retirement, and only checks that the file exists.
- **`/agent/[handle]` answers 200 but cannot work.** Its only data fetch is `/api/agent-score` (`page.tsx:88`), which was deleted on 2026-05-29 in 4b18901f (GET returned 404 at 06:30:35Z). The embeddable `/badge/agent/*` renders 'score unavailable'.
- **An internal contract-deploy page is public.** `/app/robinhood-router` (marked '(internal)' and noindex) deploys RobinhoodSwapRouter and runs test swaps on Robinhood Chain 4663 from the connected wallet. `app.blueagent.dev/robinhood-router` answers 200.
- **The waitlist gate is not armed.** `/app/cron` answered 200 to a request without cookies; an armed gate would send a 307 to `/waitlist`. No page returned 401 or 403.
- **Sitemap:** 118 `<loc>` entries (4 static and 114 `/app/hub/<id>`). 115 of them are 301s, all measured: section 6 probed all 114 `/app/hub/<id>` URLs (each 301, and every target on `app.blueagent.dev` answered 200; scratchpad `s6-probe-results.txt`, `s6-redirect-results.txt`), and 1a measured `/hub` (301). `/track`, the `/docs/*` subpages and `/app/hood` are not listed.
- **Docs pages missing from the docs sidebar (DOC_NAV):** `/docs/api`, `/docs/commands` and `/docs/cli`.
- **Where the code really lives.** Folder line counts mislead:
  - `src/app/chat` is a redirect stub, yet it holds 13,046 lines of live Blue Chat UI.
  - The archived `src/app/app/bank` (8,868 lines) supplies `BankClient.tsx` (3,495 lines) to the live `/app/wallet`.
  - The shadowed `src/app/hub` (5,617 lines) supplies `HubView.tsx` (2,289 lines) to every `/app/hub` page.
  - Per-page line counts are in the rows, under "LOC in own dir".

**API routes.** There are 142 committed route files under `apps/web/src/app/api` (141 `route.ts` and 1 `route.tsx`), totalling 21,779 LOC, of which `chat/route.ts` alone is 3,203. All 142 answer in production. For 127, the OPTIONS `Allow` header lists exactly the committed exports (0 mismatches); the other 15 were confirmed by route-specific GET JSON.

A caution on method: on this host, OPTIONS returns 204 even for a path that does not exist. The control path `/api/zz-audit-nonexistent-20260929` got OPTIONS 204 and GET 404. Only the `Allow` header tells a real route apart.

| caller evidence | routes |
|---|---|
| called from inside the app | 85 |
| documented public API or external webhook | 12 |
| scheduled in `apps/web/vercel.json` | 7 |
| no reachable caller in the repo | 38 |
| **total committed** | **142** |
| local-only (3 untracked flash routes, all 404 in prod, plus local edits to `/api/chat`) | 4 rows |
| referenced somewhere but not deployed (404) | 4 rows |

- **Anonymous LLM spend, or paid tools run with no charge:**
  - `/api/agent-registry/submit` has no auth, no rate limit and no price. It makes at least 2 Virtuals calls per request and still runs 'MiroShark' and 'Aeon' prompts. It is the same class of surface as the retired collab-builder.
  - `/api/chat` never debits a request that has no `address` on the server; the guest quota lives in localStorage.
  - `/api/console` runs the 5 priced commands with no charge, behind an IP rate limit only.
  - `/api/hub/hosted/test` is an anonymous LLM dry-run.
  - `/api/hood/trade/quote` and `/prepare` run the $0.05 catalog tools `rh-stock-swap-quote` and `-prepare` for free.
- **DCA cannot work in prod.** `/api/dca/whoami` reports `KEEPER_MASTER_KEY:false`, and `cron/dca-executor` is not scheduled anywhere.
- **Two cron routes accept a header any caller can send.** `cron/b20hub-distribute` (:65) and `cron/dca-executor` (:36) treat the mere presence of `x-vercel-cron` as auth, and both lead to on-chain writes signed by a keeper. Not tested, because testing would touch funds.
- **Moralis fails as a 200 here, not a 502.** `/api/wallet/transactions` returns HTTP 200 with body error `moralis 401`. `/api/wallet/holdings` silently falls back to `source: discovery` with `partial: true`.
- **Packages call a path that does not exist.** `/api/tools/<id>` (404) is hardcoded in `packages/vercel-ai/src/client.ts:39`, `packages/langchain/blueagent_langchain/client.py:46` and the published `@blueagent/agentkit@0.1.0`.

**Other route files.** Besides the 77 pages and 142 API routes, `apps/web/src/app` holds 15 committed route files that are neither (`git ls-files apps/web/src/app` minus `app/api`, filtered to `route.ts`, `opengraph-image.tsx`, `robots.ts` and `sitemap.ts`): 5 `.well-known` routes, the `/badge/[type]/[handle]` SVG endpoint, 7 `opengraph-image.tsx` banners, `robots.ts` and `sitemap.ts`. **Total: 77 + 142 + 15 = 234 committed route files.** All 15 were requested in production in the fixer pass, or reused from an earlier fetch for the badge.

- 14 of the 15 answer. The exception is `app/hub/opengraph-image.tsx`, which never serves: the `/hub/*` 301 sends its URL to the app host, where `opengraph-image` is treated as a tool id and the page says 'Tool not found'.
- Every `og:image` on the app host points at `https://blueagent.dev/app/...`, so each share preview costs a 301 hop before the image loads.
- `/.well-known/farcaster.json` is served by the route, not by the `public/` copy. Production returns compact one-line JSON (847 B), while the committed `public/.well-known/farcaster.json` is pretty-printed (963 B). Their content is equal.
- The badge answers 200, but it shows 'score unavailable', because its data source `/api/agent-score` is deleted.

**A second x402 backend outside `apps/web`.** `services/x402-server` is committed at HEAD. It is an Express app with `/healthz` and `/ecosystem-digest`, a Bankr LLM client (`llm.bankr.bot`, the host of the Bankr account that CLAUDE.md records as banned, 403 when last measured there on 2026-09-18), a launchd plist set to `RunAtLoad` and `KeepAlive`, and a README that describes it as running on a Mac Mini behind a Cloudflare Tunnel, reached through `x402.bankr.bot`. It is 460 committed lines, 233 of them in `src/`. It is not running on the audit host (section 5), and whether it runs anywhere could not be determined. `TUNNEL_BASE_URL`, which is set in production and has 0 code readers, is the name `scripts/setup-tunnel.sh:96-105` tells the operator to use for this backend's tunnel URL.

## 2 · Tools

### Counts, each from its own source

The brief asked for the count taken from `mcp/route.ts` and the count taken from "the schema list", separately. "The schema list" can mean two things, and both are answered:

- **`/api/mcp` manifest against the `/api/mcp` callable set: 19 and 19, no disagreement.** The manifest `MCP_TOOLS` (`mcp-tools.ts:111`, aliased at `route.ts:109`) is 8 `blue_`, 10 `hub_` and 1 `b20_`. The `tools/call` dispatcher reaches exactly the same 19 names: `HUB_MAP` 11, `CONSOLE_MAP` 2, `B20_ENCODE_TOOLS` 1 and 5 inline branches. The symmetric difference is empty, and production `tools/list` returns the same 19.
- **`@blueagent/core` `TOOL_SCHEMAS` (published in core 1.0.4): 32 keys, 20 of them not catalog ids.** That is a disagreement. Its web twin, `apps/web/src/lib/tool-inputs.ts`, has 26 keys (14 not catalog ids) and zero importers.

| list | count | not in the 114-id catalog | source measured |
|---|---|---|---|

### Price, chat exposure, upstream

- **Price.** The x402 route charges `AGENT_TOOLS[].price` (`route.ts:27-36`). `priceUSDC`, the chat credit cost (2,000 credits per USDC, rounded to 10) and every `.well-known` manifest are derived from that field, and all agree for 114 of 114 ids. The deployed app has no second price source that disagrees. 109 ids are priced above $0 and 5 are $0.00: blue-doctor, hood-live, hood-track-record, rh-rwa-verify and rh-token-scan. The prices that drift are in the client packages (section 6 and the 2a rows).
- **Surfaced in chat.** In the committed chat code, 41 catalog ids are direct chat tools, going through `TOOL_ENDPOINT` to `/api/x402/<id>`. `hub_builder_score` and `hub_crypto_rpc` point at routes outside the catalog. All 114 ids, counted from the `chat` field of the 2b rows: 41 direct chat tools, + token-price (through `hub_token_price` to `/api/token-price`, not the catalog handler), + 6 free through the optional 'Blue Hub' connector's preloaded `hub_*` and `blue_doctor` names (pool-scan, blue-doctor, wallet-holdings, gas-tracker, wallet-risk, liquidity-depth), + 66 reachable only through paid `blue_call`, and only if the user attaches that connector = 114. The earlier sentence here said 'the other 72 ids can be reached only through `blue_call`'; 6 of those 72 are reachable free. 11 catalog ids are preloaded in the `/api/mcp` manifest. The 11 Blue Chat names outside `TOOL_ENDPOINT` (55 names, 44 mapped) and the 8 `/api/mcp` names that are not catalog ids now have their own 2b rows, prefixed `chat` and `MCP`.
- **Upstream liveness (keyless probes, 2026-09-29 around 06:27Z):**
  - Live: DexScreener; GeckoTerminal (Base and Robinhood networks); DefiLlama (TVL, protocols, yields); GitHub; `mainnet.base.org` (eth_chainId 0x2105); the Robinhood Chain RPC (0x1237).
  - Blocked or failing from the audit host: RH Blockscout returned a Cloudflare 403 twice; `eth.llamarpc.com` (basename resolution in chat `check_authorization`) returned HTTP 525 (fixer pass, 10:09:23Z). Vercel's egress was not measured.
  - Keyed, so not observable: Etherscan v2 and 0x (keyless probe 401, 10:06:40Z).
  - Observed answering in production, indirectly (fixer pass): **Virtuals** answered at 2026-09-29T03:24:47Z. brief-worker attached a Virtuals brief to arrow #0660, 21 s after the arrow fired (`/api/hood/arrows` `brief.fetched_at`, `llm_provider` virtuals, attempt status success). It also returned 402 'Insufficient credits' twice in 7 days, on the briefs of arrows fired 2026-09-25T07:34Z and 2026-09-28T01:49Z; the current balance is unknown. **KV** answered: `/api/usage` returned 200 with 119 counters (06:28:37Z), `/api/stats` reported `countersUnreadable: 0`, and `/api/hood/health` showed a heartbeat 298 s old (06:20:44Z). So 33 catalog tools whose only unobserved upstream was Virtuals or KV moved from the keyed-unobservable class to 'observed indirectly', together with the two method rows. 19 catalog tools stay unobservable, all because of Etherscan v2 or RH Blockscout, and 3 of the new non-catalog rows join them (0x twice, `eth.llamarpc.com` once). None of those tools' own code paths was run.
  - Dead: Moralis, which answers 401 'Your Moralis Free usage is paused'.
- **Upstream counts across the 114 ids, after verification:**
  - Virtuals LLM 64, all priced above $0.
  - GeckoTerminal 36, DexScreener 30, Base RPC 21 or more, DefiLlama 15, Chainlink via RH RPC 16.
  - Aeon KV 15, Etherscan getsourcecode 13, RH RPC 14 or more, Moralis 9, GitHub 6, RH Blockscout 4, compute only 4.
  - The measurer had LLM 65, DexScreener 31, DefiLlama 16, Etherscan 14 and RH RPC 11 or more. The verifier removed the upstreams safe-trending had wrongly inherited from helper imports, and added the RH-RPC code hint that 3 tools call.

### Moralis blast radius

Moralis is dead for our key. Production MCP calls returned 502 `UPSTREAM_PLAN_PAUSED: Moralis returned HTTP 401: Your Moralis Free usage is paused` at about 06:22Z, and again at 06:46:27Z.

- 9 catalog tools depend on Moralis, and **all 9 are priced above $0**: $1.02 for the set.
- Only 2 of them fail loud. The other 7 return HTTP 200 on absent data, and the x402 route settles USDC on a 200 (`route.ts:539/566`).
- After verification, Moralis kills 8 of the tools and degrades 1 (token-alpha).

| id | price | behaviour while Moralis returns 401 | x402 caller charged? | in chat | in /api/mcp manifest |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| wallet-holdings | $0.02 | 502, fails loud (l.128) | no | only through the connector (no debit) | yes, `hub_wallet_holdings` (dead) |
| wallet-risk | $0.05 | 502, fails loud; LLM never reached (l.112-114) | no | only through the connector (no debit) | yes, `hub_wallet_risk` (dead) |
| token-distribution | $0.05 | 200; every number null, verdict "CAUTION" (l.57) | yes | through blue_call only | no |
| base-activity-score | $0.05 | 200; score 0, tier "Newcomer", `data_source: "Moralis (live)"` (l.13, l.56) | yes | through blue_call only | no |
| token-alpha | $0.15 | 200; degraded, not killed. For address input, a transfer count of 0 goes to the LLM; price and liquidity still come from DexScreener | yes | through blue_call only | no |
| airdrop-check | $0.10 | 200; eligibilityScore null and activityLevel "INACTIVE" on every call (l.100-104) | yes | direct tool `hub_airdrop`; 200 credits debited before the handler runs | no |
| whale-tracker | $0.10 | 200; "NEUTRAL", source labelled Basescan (l.124, l.130) | yes | direct tool `hub_whale_tracker`; 200 credits | no |
| aml-screen | $0.25 | 200; INSUFFICIENT_DATA or NO_HISTORY, source labelled Basescan (l.107) | yes | direct tool `hub_aml`; 500 credits | no |
| whale-copy-signal | $0.25 | 200; signal "PASS" (l.128) | yes | direct tool `hub_whale_signal`; 500 credits | no |

- 2 of the 11 catalog ids preloaded in the `/api/mcp` manifest are dead: `hub_wallet_holdings` and `hub_wallet_risk`. `hub_wallet_risk` is one of the 'safety five' that the HUB_MAP comment says an agent must reach for before money moves.
- `blue_doctor`'s known-issues text says 'wallet-holdings, wallet-risk and the onchain-activity tools return 502 by design'. That is true of 2 of the 9.
- Chat debits credits before the handler runs, and never refunds a tool debit: `route.ts:315` calls `spend()` before l.360 calls `handler()`. So "you were not charged" on a 502 is true only on the x402 USDC path.
- Outside the catalog, the free chat tool `check_wallet` falls back to keyless on-chain discovery, so it is degraded, not killed. `/api/wallet/transactions`, `/api/wallet/holdings`, `/api/wallet/net-worth` and `lib/pledge/sources.ts:104` also read Moralis.

### Other tool findings

- **Aeon.** 15 handlers read Aeon KV, under keys with different writers (`git grep setAeonOutput HEAD`, fixer pass):
  - `aeon:deep-research` (5 handlers): the only writer is `research-loop` (`route.ts:385`), unscheduled on 2026-09-05 and not run for at least 14 days (section 5). The null path holds.
  - `aeon:digest` (launch-simulator-1): no writer anywhere. The null path holds.
  - `aeon:narrative-tracker` and `aeon:token-movers` (9 handlers): `research-loop` never wrote these. The only writer is `POST /api/aeon-feed` (`route.ts:87`, token-gated, 26 h TTL). An off-repo GitHub job, `madebyshun/blueagent-aeon` (section 5), runs both skills every day and posts each result to its `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL` secret. That secret was created on 2026-05-27T15:51:35Z, 5.5 h after commit fb57abda added `/api/aeon-feed` with a header telling the operator to set exactly that secret to `https://blueagent.dev/api/aeon-feed?token=...`. The secret's value was not read, and the job discards the response (`> /dev/null`), so whether these keys are live in production is undetermined. **For these 9 paid tools the null path is not established.** If the job does post there, they serve Aeon text refreshed daily, filed under whichever key the route's content-sniffing regexes pick (`aeon-feed/route.ts:31-41`).
  - As the measurer found for a null key: 9 handlers silently put placeholder text such as 'Base ecosystem' into the prompt. 2 tell the model to label its output 'model estimate', and the 3 launch-simulators drop the section. `base-grant-finder` reads the key and then throws the value away (l.85).
- **Paid tools that promise delivery nothing performs.** `rh-stock-alert` ($0.10) returns mode 'armed_kv' and says 'Poller will notify when it crosses'. `rh-rwa-dca` ($0.20) writes `rh-dca:*` for 'a downstream cron'. No committed file reads either key family.
- **The MiroShark skill-file injection in `llm.ts` is unreachable from every handler.** The 20 files carrying the 'You are MiroShark' prefix all call `callLLM`, which does no injection. CLAUDE.md says the compatibility shims have '~46 legacy importers'; the measured number of real importers is 2 (`b20-analyze` and `lib/hub-hosted.ts`). The rest define their own local wrappers that happen to share the shim's name and call `callLLM`.

## 3 · `/app/scheduled`: the four questions

**There is no `/app/scheduled` route.** In production, `blueagent.dev/app/scheduled` 301s to `app.blueagent.dev/scheduled`, which 301s to `blueagent.dev/scheduled`, which returns 404. The Scheduled feature lives at `/app/cron`:

- It is served at `app.blueagent.dev/cron` (200).
- It is linked from the app nav: `AppShell.tsx:137` `{ id: "cron", href: "/cron", icon: IconCron }`, with the label `i18n/en.ts:151` `cron: "Scheduled"`.
- The page is a 25-line wrapper around `CronPanel.tsx` (608 lines).
- The feature has no tests: no file under `apps/web/scripts` imports `cron-schedule` or `scheduled-tasks`.

### Q1. Can a user express an arbitrary cron, or only pick from presets?

**Presets only: daily or weekly, at HH:MM, in the user's IANA time zone.**

- UI, `apps/web/src/app/chat/components/CronPanel.tsx:27-30`: `const SCHEDULES ... = [ { value: "daily",  label: "Every day" }, { value: "weekly", label: "Every week" }, ];`, plus an `<input type="time">` at :226-231.
- Model, `apps/web/src/lib/cron-schedule.ts:31-32`: `export type Cadence = "daily" | "weekly";`
- Server, `apps/web/src/lib/scheduled-tasks.ts:142`: `const schedule: Cadence = r.schedule === "weekly" ? "weekly" : "daily";`. Any other value, including a cron string, silently becomes daily. The verifier ran the code: a cron string placed in `time` becomes 09:00 (`parseHHMM`, `cron-schedule.ts:119-121`).
- No cron parser exists: `git grep` for cron-parser, croner, node-cron and parseExpression finds nothing.
- The user cannot choose the weekday of a weekly task. It fires today at HH:MM if that time is still ahead, and otherwise 7 days out (corrected in verification).
- Schedules are created only from `CronPanel.tsx:132`. No chat or LLM path creates one.

### Q2. Can a schedule carry a condition, or is it purely time-based?

**Purely time-based.**

- Executor, `apps/web/src/app/api/cron/user-tasks/route.ts:180-182`: `for (const task of tasks) { if (!task.active) continue; if (!Number.isFinite(task.nextAt) || task.nextAt > now) continue;`. After that it always runs the LLM pipeline, always debits credits and always stores the result (:192-216).
- Record, `scheduled-tasks.ts:84-101`: `export interface ScheduledTask { id; label; schedule: Cadence; time; tz; prompt; tier; active; nextAt; lastRun?; lastResult?; lastError?; pausedReason? }`. There is no condition, trigger or threshold field. The only branch not based on time is the auto-pause on insufficient credits (:200-209).
- A condition can only be written as prose in the prompt. Nothing suppresses delivery or the charge when the condition is false.
- Elsewhere in the code, unwired: the paid tool `rh-stock-alert` ($0.10) saves `{threshold_usd, direction, recipient, webhook_url}` to KV `rh-alert:*` (`rh-stock-alert.ts:17-25`), and nothing reads it.

### Q3. What delivery targets exist?

**In-app only.**

- In-app: `user-tasks/route.ts:196-198` runs `if (outcome.kind === "ok") { task.lastResult = outcome.text;`, then :225 `await writeOwnerTasks(wallet, tasks);` writes KV `crons:w:<wallet>`. The browser pulls the result when the page opens (`use-schedule-sync.ts:110` `fetch("/api/chat/schedule", { cache: "no-store" })`), with no polling or push, and shows it in the panel's LAST RESULT row.
- Webhook: absent. No field, no input, no sender.
- Telegram: absent for schedules. A sender exists (`lib/telegram/bot.ts`, used by the Blue Hood alert-drain cron), and so does a wallet-to-Telegram map (`lib/blue-hood/watchlist.ts:480` `tgUserForAddress`).
- Web Push: absent for schedules. A VAPID sender exists (`lib/blue-hood/push.ts`), but its subscriptions are keyed by endpoint hash and sent as a broadcast, with no wallet mapping.
- Email: absent, and no email sender exists anywhere in `apps/web/src`. `git grep` for resend.com, nodemailer, sendgrid and postmark finds nothing.
- The panel says, at `CronPanel.tsx:341`, 'Background tasks run server-side and post results into Blue Chat'. No code does this: nothing creates a chat message from a run.

### Q4. Does a schedule run server-side when the tab is closed?

**Only if the user opts in. By default, runs are driven by the browser.**

- Server path, registered: `apps/web/vercel.json` has `{"path":"/api/cron/user-tasks","schedule":"*/5 * * * *"}` (added in 0cfed5c6, 2026-09-04), which calls `user-tasks/route.ts:246` `export async function GET(req: NextRequest)`. It runs only tasks the user switched to Background after signing in with the wallet (SIWE). There are two caps: at most 10 tasks per wallet, and at most 8 runs per tick across all users (:76 `const MAX_RUNS_PER_TICK = 8;`). An 11th task is silently dropped by the server and never fires.
- Client path, the default: `ChatContext.tsx:480-487` runs `const due = crons.filter(isDue); ... for (const c of due) await runCron(c.id);`. In `storage.ts:131-137`, `isDue` returns false for background tasks and ignores `time`, so a foreground task fires on page open once 24 h or 7 d has passed. `types.ts:110-117` declares `background?: boolean`, 'Off by default and requires a signed-in session'.
- Whether the server path fired in the last 7 days could not be determined. Vercel runtime logs returned 403, the state is in KV, and the unauthenticated GET returns 401 before doing any work.

### What this means for the next phase: new work or wiring

- **Arbitrary cron: new work.** It needs a parser, a UI and server validation; today the server rewrites anything it does not recognise to daily.
- **Conditions: new work** in the schedule model: a field, an evaluator, and a way to suppress delivery and the charge when the condition is false. Related pieces exist but are not wired up:
  - rh-stock-alert's threshold shape, which has no evaluator.
  - The DCA frequency model, whose executor has never been scheduled.
  - So the codebase has 3 scheduling or condition models (Blue Chat crons, DCA schedules, rh-stock-alert thresholds), and only the first has a registered executor. Part of this phase is consolidation.
- **Delivery:**
  - In-app already exists.
  - Telegram is wiring: both the sender and the wallet-to-Telegram map exist.
  - Per-wallet push needs a new mapping.
  - Webhook and email are new: no sender for either exists anywhere.
- **Server-side execution exists and is registered.** What is missing is evidence and safety, not infrastructure:
  - No observable proof that it has ever fired.
  - A double-charge risk, derived from the code and not observed. 8 runs at 95 s each is 760 s, against a 300 s `maxDuration`, and results are saved per wallet only after that wallet's loop. A tick killed mid-loop can leave runs that were already debited due to run again.
  - The silent 10-task cap.
  - `/api/cron/run`, which the tick calls, is unauthenticated by design and bills whatever wallet address the request body names.

## 4 · Metering

**Plainly: nothing converts credits into money.** Money comes into credits through exactly one route, and credits also arrive through a few free grants. Credits leave only as spend, or as a refund that is itself paid in credits. The "USDC credit packs coming soon" notice has nothing pending behind it: the pack purchase it announces has been live since 2026-08-06, and the string was added a day after that.

- **What the ledger stores.** One KV row per wallet, `ledger:<addr>`, written with no TTL (`credit-ledger.ts:62-125, :163, :170-174`). It holds:
  - `spent`, and `topup`, which is one number for both purchases and free grants.
  - The last 50 events, each a `spend`, `topup` or `refund`.
  - The UTC-day bucket (`dailyDay`, `dailySpent`) and `freeSpent`.
  - `accrued`, which is always 0.
  - Hub x402 USDC payments are kept in separate books: per-payer `spend:<addr>` receipts (90-day TTL) and the aggregate `x402:settle:*` meter. They never touch the ledger, but both rails pay into the same wallet (`payments.ts:26 == x402-payee.ts:54`).
- **What it counts.**
  - Chat is charged per message by preset, not per token. Measured in `/api/chat/models`: fast 10, free 0, balanced 50, deep 200, private 30, flash 10, grok 60, search 60. The default tier `pro`, and any tier it does not recognise, costs 50.
  - A chat tool call is charged round10(price in USD x 2000) credits. The charge happens before the handler runs, carries no ref and is never refunded, even when the handler fails with a 502. The USDC path, by contrast, settles only after success.
  - A chat message that produces zero output tokens is refunded, in credits.
- **Who is counted.**
  - Both debit paths key on an address the client supplies, with no authentication, and both fail open: if the ledger errors or the internal key is missing, the request is served free.
  - A request with no address is never debited on the server, on any preset. Guests are metered only in the browser's localStorage: 100 credits over a rolling 24 h.
  - Any syntactically valid address gets 500 free credits per UTC day, with no proof of ownership.
  - MCP calls (`internalX402Headers()`) are charged neither credits nor USDC. `blue_call` is the one MCP tool that settles real USDC.
- **Money to credits: one route.** `POST /api/credits/purchase` grants floor(usdc x 2000) credits.
  - Corrected in verification: it credits any USDC Transfer log from the body address to the treasury, not only a `transfer()` call. The treasury is also the x402 payee, so x402 settlements can be redeemed as credits.
  - Its idempotency marker lasts 1 year, and the write that makes the marker permanent can fail silently.
  - Every USDC inflow ever into 0x0295 is self (section 7). However many `purchase:usdc` events exist (anywhere from 0 to 4 is possible), they are backed by self money.
  - At most one intentional pack purchase has happened: $5 from the project deployer 0xD5C1 on 2026-08-08, relayed through contract 0xdb9b1e94.... Its ledger shows topup 10000, which is 5 x 2000.
- **Credits to money: nothing.** There is no withdraw, payout, cash-out or redeem path (`credit-ledger.ts:94, :525-598`; a code search finds only comments).
  - The only other money-like accrual, `builder:earned:<wallet>`, holds 90% of a hosted-tool x402 settlement in USDC micro-units, and it has no payout code either.
  - A fiat rail does exist, but it does not touch credits: `/api/onramp/session` (Coinbase Onramp and Offramp) moves fiat to and from USDC in the user's own wallet.
- **Free credits far outnumber paid ones, and share the same field.**
  - 29 airdrop claims at 1,000 each put 29,000 free credits in `topup`, against at most 10,000 purchased.
  - A claim needs no signature: any address in the body qualifies, limited to 3 per IP.
  - The spend console and BlueBank still label spending from that pool as "paid top-ups ≈ $".
- **Claims to users that the code contradicts.**
  - `/docs/credits` says 'you never spend chat credits on a tool call'. That is false: the deployer's ledger shows 400-credit `tool:token-pick-signal` debits.
  - `/app/plans` says the top-up is 'Coinbase x402'. It is a plain ERC-20 transfer, verified from the receipt.
  - The landing page's 'Failed call refunded' is true only for chat turns with zero tokens.
  - 'Paying in $BLUEAGENT arrives after the token relaunch' has no code behind it.
- **Aggregate usage.** This includes self traffic, and it is a floor because history is capped. 23 wallets have spent 12,590 credits across 117 messages. The credits block on `/stats` has no `ok` flag, so a failure would show up as zeros.

## 5 · Background work

The committed `apps/web/vercel.json` registers 7 crons. 13 route files are deployed under `api/cron`. There is also 1 scheduled GitHub workflow, 1 event-driven one and 1 manual-only one. Outside this repo, the fixer pass found one more scheduler that runs every day under the BlueAgent name (`madebyshun/blueagent-aeon`), and checked every other one it could reach: other repos, this host's launchd and crontab, and the local Claude scheduled tasks.

The table keeps three cases apart: configured and observed running, configured with no observable artifact, and not configured. Silence from a configured job is not evidence that it ran, and not evidence that it did not.

- **A BlueAgent-named job still runs every day outside this repo.** The repo retired Aeon, and research-loop has not run for at least 14 days. Both are true here, but `madebyshun/blueagent-aeon` (a fork of `aeonfun/aeon`) ran 7 days out of 7:
  - It runs morning-brief, github-trending, token-pick, token-movers, defi-monitor, narrative-tracker, security-digest and, weekly, deal-flow. Its own config marks 5 of those `enabled: false`, and the schedule workflow dispatches them anyway.
  - It runs Claude through Claude Code (`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`). The config's gateway is `direct`, so the `BANKR_LLM_KEY` -> `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` export in its workflow is never taken.
  - It delivers to Telegram (its `memory/MEMORY.md:7` names t.me/blueagent_hub, the community in CLAUDE.md) and to `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL`. The Slack and SendGrid branches have no secrets.
  - Whether it writes production KV through `/api/aeon-feed` is undetermined; see section 2, Aeon.
- Vercel's own cron registry and invocation history could not be read: the Vercel MCP listed the project but returned 403 for `get_project` and for runtime logs. In the table, "registered" means "in vercel.json", not "confirmed by Vercel".
- **RH archive:** covers 2026-08-10T03:05Z to 2026-09-29T06:00Z with 1,160 hourly points for each of 24 tickers. It has no TTL, 44 missing hours (all between 2026-08-19 and 08-27), and no watchdog.
- **Base archive:** covers 2026-08-28T09:20Z to 2026-09-29T06:00Z with 766 points, 0 gaps and no TTL. Points per ticker range from 766 down to 14 (SPCX).

## 6 · Dead surface

- **Routes with zero callers.**
  - 38 deployed API routes have no reachable caller in the repo (section 1b): `/api/acp/arrows`, `/api/acp/drift`, `/api/acp/execution-plan`, `/api/acp/revenue`, `/api/acp/track-record`, `/api/acp/verify`, `/api/activity`, `/api/aeon-feed`, `/api/base-snapshot`, `/api/credits/topup`, `/api/cron/b20hub-distribute`, `/api/cron/blue-hood/purge`, `/api/cron/blue-hood/seed-test-arrow`, `/api/cron/dca-executor`, `/api/cron/research-loop`, `/api/dca/cancel`, `/api/dca/list`, `/api/dca/logs`, `/api/dca/whoami`, `/api/hood/alerts`, `/api/hood/base-series`, `/api/hood/chat/card/[id]`, `/api/hood/chat/recent`, `/api/hood/dislocation`, `/api/hood/dislocation/spread`, `/api/hood/llm-health`, `/api/hood/series`, `/api/hub/builders/[address]/tools`, `/api/hub/community/[slug]/invoke`, `/api/hub/community/jobs/[id]`, `/api/robinhood/prepare`, `/api/robinhood/receipt`, `/api/signals`, `/api/status`, `/api/usage/daily`, `/api/v1/healthz`, `/api/webhook/aeon`, `/api/x402/.well-known/bazaar`.
  - 13 pages render but are linked from nowhere (section 1a): `/agent/[handle]`, `/app/bridge`, `/app/hub/builders/[address]`, `/app/hub/stats`, `/app/radar`, `/app/rewards`, `/app/robinhood-router`, `/app/tasks`, `/app/trade`, `/builder/[handle]`, `/docs/cli`, `/stats`, `/waitlist`.
  - "Zero callers" comes from a static scan of the repo. No request logs were read, so external callers cannot be ruled out.
  - Referenced but absent: `/api/tools/{id}` (hardcoded in three client packages), `/api/tool/[toolId]`, `/api/launches` and `/api/cron/feed/daily`.
- **Env vars.**
  - 106 names have committed code readers, 68 of them in the deployed app.
  - 16 names are set (in `.env.local` or the 2026-09-25 production pull) with zero readers. They include `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `XAI_API_KEY` in production (keys for non-Virtuals LLM providers; hard rule 3 says to use Virtuals for all AI calls), `RESEND_API_KEY`, `QSTASH_*`, and `VICTUALS_MODEL`. That last one is a typo of `VIRTUALS_MODEL`, so the local model override is silently ignored.
  - 41 names read at runtime appear in neither source. The pull is provably partial: it lacks `VIRTUALS_API_KEY`, yet production reports the key as configured. So for those 41, "absent" means "not visible locally", not "unset".
- **Three CLAUDE.md env claims, measured:**
  - `VENICE_INFERENCE_KEY` is not dead: it has 4 runtime readers.
  - The banned Bankr key still has 3 readers in the committed `services/x402-server`, a second x402 backend with its own launchd plist (sections 1c and 5).
  - `PAYMENT_WALLET` has 0 readers, as CLAUDE.md states.
- **The Bankr storefront is still live and selling.**
  - At `x402.bankr.bot/0xf31f59.../<id>`, 30 of 31 probed ids answered 402, with payTo 0x8AEE6210 (Bankr's router, named BANKR_WALLET in commit b3e3844e).
  - 18 of those ids are not in the catalog, for example quantum-premium at $1.50, launch-advisor at $3.00 and tax-report at $2.00.
  - On-chain, the storefront has settled 57 payments (22.54 USDC) forwarded to 0xf31f59, the last on 2026-09-19 (section 7).
  - CLAUDE.md's 'no Bankr in the payment path' is true for blueagent.dev only. The Bankr account is banned (403), but this storefront was never retired.
  - **Its source is a separate repo, not the deleted apps/api (fixer pass).** The 31 service keys of `bankr.x402.json` in `madebyshun/blueagent-x402-services` are exactly the 31 ids probed. All 30 live prices match that file. The deleted apps/api manifest shares only 12 of the ids and prices 5 of them differently; on all 5, the live price is the x402-services price. The repo's `deploy.yml` runs `bankr x402 deploy <svc>` on each push that touches `x402/**` or `bankr.x402.json`. Its last run was 2026-05-15, a success at the commit that is still HEAD. It is still active. **Retiring the storefront therefore means that repo and that workflow, not anything in this one.**
  - **What a payer receives, from the handler source.** All 31 handlers call `https://llm.bankr.bot/v1/messages`, the host of the banned account. When that call fails, 29 return HTTP 500, while risk-gate returns HTTP 200 with `decision: BLOCK, riskScore: 100` and circuit-breaker returns HTTP 200 with `decision: PAUSE, riskScore: 80`. Whether the storefront's LLM key is the banned account's, and whether Bankr settles a payment on a 500, could not be determined.
- **Published packages.** The package list comes from the registry (fixer pass): `registry.npmjs.org/-/v1/search?text=%40blueagent` returns 10 `@blueagent/*` packages, all maintained by madebyshun, plus the unrelated `blueagent@1.0.0` from 2015. The first pass covered 9 of them and missed `@blueagent/x402-guard`. Tool ids checked against the 114-id catalog:

| package (version) | tool ids | not in catalog | calls |
|---|---|---|---|
| `@blueagent/skill@0.4.1` (latest) | 42 | 0 | `/api/v1/<id>` (exists) |
| `@blueagent/agentkit@0.1.0` (latest) | 32 | 20 | `/api/tools/<id>` (404, so all 32 are unreachable) |
| `@blueagent/cli@1.3.14` (latest) | 29 | 17 | the Bankr storefront |
| `@blueagent/sdk@1.1.2`, `@blueagent/skill@1.1.3` (stranded above latest) | 31 each | 19 each | the Bankr storefront, paying with the user's key |
| `@blueagent/agentkit@1.1.1` (stranded) | through sdk 1.1.2 | 19 | the Bankr storefront |
| `@blueagent/builder@0.1.15` (latest) | 5 | 2 | doc examples |
| `@blueagent/core@1.0.4` (latest) TOOL_SCHEMAS | 32 | 20 | 0 importers |
| `@blueagent/x402@0.1.0` (latest) | 1 | 0 | example |
| `@blueagent/x402-guard@1.0.0` (latest; fixer pass) | 4 | 1 (phishing-scan) | the Bankr storefront, paying with a caller-supplied key; no source in any repo |

- **Retired products still sold on npm:**
  - The official installer, `blueagent.dev/setup.sh`, installs `cli@1.3.14`, which pulls `builder@0.1.15`, which pulls `core@0.1.2`. The 5 core commands in that build call the banned `llm.bankr.bot` host. The Virtuals fix (core 1.0.4, cli 1.3.15, builder 0.2.0) exists locally but was never published.
  - `tasks@0.1.0` consists entirely of the microtask marketplace, retired on 2026-09-02.
  - `cli@1.3.14` sells Microtasks, a Clanker launch and 4 quantum tools.
  - `reputation@0.1.1` calls the banned LLM host and links to `api.blueagent.dev`, which returns 404.
  - `x402-guard@1.0.0` advertises 'BlueAgent x402 Cloud — 31 AI-agent security and intelligence tools' (README:100) and hardcodes 4 storefront endpoints (`src/guard.ts:9-12`): honeypot-check, phishing-scan, contract-trust and risk-gate. Three of those names are catalog ids, but not one call reaches blueagent.dev. There is no source for it in this repo or in `blueagent-x402-services`, the repo its metadata names. It had 10 downloads in 2026-08-29..09-27.
  - `skill@0.4.1` still says 'Blue Agent + Aeon + MiroShark', though Aeon and MiroShark were retired on 2026-09-27. So does HEAD `packages/skill/src/index.ts:485`.
- **Version drift:**
  - Versions stranded above npm latest: skill 1.0.0 to 1.1.3 (6 versions), sdk 1.0.1 to 1.1.2 (5) and agentkit 1.1.1. So `^1.0.0` resolves to the old builds that pay the Bankr storefront.
  - Local sdk 1.2.0 and agentkit 1.3.0 sit on that abandoned 1.x line.
  - Unpublished local versions ahead of npm: cli 1.3.15, builder 0.2.0, reputation 0.2.0.
  - Never published: `@blueagent/vercel-ai` (which is not marked private) and `blueagent-langchain`.
- **Published manifests on prod match the 114-id catalog:**
  - openapi has 114 paths, pricing lists 109 plus 5 free, bazaar has 114, ai-plugin and llms.txt say '114 / 109 / 5' and MCP 19, farcaster has 114, and the sitemap has 114 `/app/hub/<id>`.
  - 117 of the 140 advertised URLs 301 to the app host, and all 117 targets answer 200.
- **The false published claims are elsewhere:**
  - `agent.json` advertises the CLI as running tools 'with x402 payment', but the CLI goes to the Bankr storefront.
  - `/api/health` still lists the Aeon webhook.
  - The claude-plugin marketplace says '15 Hub tools', while `/api/mcp` serves 10 `hub_` tools.

## 7 · Detailed payer table

The verdict and the per-surface summary are at the top of this file. The rows below cover every payer and payee measured, grouped by what the evidence supports:

- **self:** ShunTr or BlueAgent-controlled, each with its link.
- **unattributed:** not tied to self, and not proven external.
- **plumbing:** facilitators, escrow, routers, and the audit sources themselves.

Skeptic A's and Skeptic B's corrections are attached to the rows they change.

- **How the self set was built.** 9 wallets, each with evidence (the "SELF set" row), plus every one of the 504 distinct `0x` literals in the committed tree. No unattributed payer matched any of them. Two wallets count as self through one hop. 0xb7b3bdf2 funded the Blue Hub deployer 1.0 USDC 6 minutes before that deployer's first x402 payment, and it received BLOCKY from 0xD5C1. 0x64d1c1 is a nonce-0 wallet that 0xD5C1 funded 51 minutes before it paid.
- **Where the skeptics moved the count:**
  - Cluster A, 5 wallets, is one operator.
  - 0x01d80ecf moves to self-leaning (weak).
  - 0xce17a154 moves from self to insider (not external).
  - B20HUB's 'zero swaps' becomes 30 swaps in legacy pools, with one self-generated fee.
  - The storefront's payments after the apps/api deletion drop from two to one. The fixer pass shows that this cut-off is the wrong one: the live storefront was never apps/api. It is deployed from `madebyshun/blueagent-x402-services`, which was never retired. So every storefront payment, 2026-09-19 included, went to a surface that is still for sale.
- **Blockscout is not a valid audit source here.** It returns 404 for the ACP completion tx, leaves out 0x0295's 0.025 inflow, and returns all-zero counters for active contracts. Every number in this section comes from RPC logs.
- **Address poisoning** targets 0x0295, 0xf31f59 and 0xb058 with lookalike senders, for example 0xD5C19875...0cf13fAB copying the deployer 0xD5C1dFc0...13FAB.
